The elements of this algebra are called graded polynomials.\nLet $f=f(x^{(t_1)}_{i_1}, \\dots, x^{(t_s)}_{i_s}) \\in F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$. We say that $f$ is a graded polynomial identity of a $T$-graded algebra $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ and write $f\\equiv 0$ if $f(a^{(t_1)}_{i_1}, \\dots, a^{(t_s)}_{i_s})=0$ for all $a^{(t_j)}_{i_j} \\in A^{(t_j)}$, $1 \\leqslant j \\leqslant s$. The set $Id^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}(A)$ of graded polynomial identities of $A$ is a graded ideal of $F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$.\n\nLet $P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n := \\langle x^{(t_1)}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{(t_2)}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{(t_n)}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid t_i \\in T, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$, $n \\in \\mathbb N$. Suppose there is a homomorphism $H \\to End_F(A)$, which we denote by $h \\mapsto (a \\mapsto ha)$. Any map $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$ has the unique homomorphic extension $\\bar\\psi \\colon F \\langle X | H \\rangle \\to A$ such that $\\bar\\psi(x_i^h)=h\\psi(x_i)$ for all $i \\in \\mathbb N$ and $h \\in H$. An $H$-polynomial $f \\in F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ is an $H$-identity of $A$ if $\\bar\\psi(f)=0$ for all maps $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$. The set $Id^{H}(A)$ of all $H$-identities of $A$ is an ideal of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$.\n\nWe denote by $P^H_n$ the space of all multilinear $H$-polynomials in $x_1, \\ldots, x_n$, $n\\in\\mathbb N$, i.e. $$P^{H}_n = \\langle x^{h_1}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{h_2}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid h_i \\in H, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X | H \\rangle.$$ Then the number $c^H_n(A):=\\dim\\left(\\frac{P^H_n}{P^H_n \\cap Id^H(A)}\\right)$ is called the $n$th codimension of polynomial $H$-identities or the $n$th $H$-codimension of $A$.\n\nFor an arbitrary semigroup $T$ one can consider the semigroup algebra $FT$ over a field $F$ which is the vector space with the formal basis $(t)_{t\\in T}$ and the multiplication induced by the one in $T$. Consider the vector space $(FT)^*$ dual to $FT$. Then $(FT)^*$ is an algebra with the multiplication defined by $(hw)(t)=h(t)w(t)$ for $h,w \\in (FT)^*$ and $t\\in T$.\n\nLet $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ be a $T$-graded algebra. We have the following natural $(FT)^*$-action on $A$: $h a^{(t)}:=h(t)a^{(t)}$ for all $h \\in (FT)^*$, $a^{(t)}\\in A^{(t)}$ and $t\\in T$. If $A$ is finite dimensional, the set $supp \\Gamma := \\lbrace t\\in T \\mid A^{(t)}\\ne 0 \\rbrace$ is finite. For any $a \\in A$, we have $a = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} a^{(t)}$, where $a^{(t)}$ is the projection of $a$ onto $A^{(t)}$. Note that $h_t a = a^{(t)}$.\nFor any $h \\in (FT)^*$ and $a \\in A$, we have $ha = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} h(t) a^{(t)} = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} h(t) h_t a$.\nThis implies that for any $h \\in (FT)^*$, the polynomial $x^h - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) x^{h_t}$ is an $(FT)^*$-identity of $A$. Indeed, for any substitution $x \\to a \\in A$, its evaluation is $ha - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) h_t a = 0$. We say that the hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ is a **Berge-$G$** if there is a bijection\n$f : E(G) \\rightarrow E(\\mathcal{H})$ such that for $e \\in E(G)$ we have $e \\subset f(e)$.\n\nWe say that a hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ contains the graph $G$ if $\\mathcal{H}$ has a Berge-$G$ as a subhypergraph. If $\\mathcal{H}$ contains no $G$, then we say that $\\mathcal{H}$ is **$G$-free**.\n\nWhen considering non-uniform hypergraphs $\\mathcal{H}$ we are interested in the value of $\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})} d(v) = \\sum_{h \\in \\mathcal{H}} |h|$ instead of $|\\mathcal{H}|$. Note that when a hypergraph is uniform than the two parameters can easily be computed from each other.\n\n**Theorem 4 (Kővári-Sós-Turán theorem)**\nIf $G$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free graph on $n$ vertices and $s \\leq t$, then there is a constant $C$ depending on $s$ and $t$ such that\n$$|E(G)| \\leq C n^{2-1/s}.$$\n\n**Lemma 1**\nLet $F$ be a graph and suppose $\\mathcal{H}$ is an $F$-free hypergraph. If every edge of $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $|V(F)|$,\nthen $|\\mathcal{H}| \\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n**Proof of Lemma 1** Let us construct a graph $H$ on the ground set of $\\mathcal{H}$ by embedding a (unique) edge into each hyperedge of $\\mathcal{H}$. By definition if $\\mathcal{H}$ is $F$-free, then $H$ must be $F$-free and thus\n$|\\mathcal{H}|=|H|\\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n\nWe can construct $H$ greedily. Take the hyperedges of $\\mathcal{H}$ in arbitrary order and for each hyperedge embed an edge that has not already been used in $H$.\nIf at some step we cannot find such an edge, then $H$ contains a complete graph of size equal to the size of the hyperedge. Obviously, such a complete graph contains a copy of $F$; a contradiction.\n\n**Lemma 2**\nA properly edge-colored $K_{s,s(s-1)(t-1)+t}$ contains a rainbow $K_{s,t}$.\n**Proof of Lemma 2** Suppose $K_{s,s(s-1)(t-1)+t}$ is properly edge-colored and let $S$ be the class of size $s$ and $T$ be the other class.\nConsider the largest rainbow $K_{s,t'}$ that uses all $s$ vertices of $S$. That is, there are $t'$ vertices in $T$ such that all edges between them and $S$ are of distinct colors. It is enough to show that $t' \\geq t$.\n\nObviously $t' \\ge 1$ as the edge-coloring is proper. For every vertex $x$ not in $K_{s,t'}$ there is a neighbor of $x$ in $S$ such that the color of the edge between them is one of the colors used in $K_{s,t'}$ (otherwise we can add $x$ to $K_{s,t'}$).\nEach vertex $y \\in S$ is adjacent to $t'$ vertices in $K_{s,t'}$, so there are $st'-t' = (s-1)t'$ colors\nthat appear in $K_{s,t'}$ not on edges incident to $y$. So at most $y$ can force $(s-1)t'$ many vertices in $T$ to not be in $K_{s,t'}$.\nTherefore, in total, there are at most $s(s-1)t'$ vertices in $T$ that are not in $K_{s,t'}$.\nSo $T$ has at most $s(s-1)t' + t'$ vertices, hence $t'\\ge t$.\n## Theorem\n**Theorem 5**\nIf $\\mathcal{H}$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph, $s \\le t$ and every hyperedge in $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $s+t$, then\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = O(n^{2-1/s}).$$\nFurthermore, there exists a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ such that each edge has size $s+t$ and\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = \\Omega( \\ex(n,K_{s,t})).$$", 'status': 'ACCEPT', 'result': {'status': 'ACCEPT', 'rejection_reason': None, 'justification': 'The prompt provides complete mathematical context including definitions, lemmas with proofs, and asks for a proof of Theorem 5. If you are unable to complete the proof, be honest and say so.\n\n## Setup\nLet $F$ be a field. Let $T$ be a semigroup. An associative algebra $A$ is $T$-graded if $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ as a vector space, and $A^{(t_1)}A^{(t_2)} \\subseteq A^{(t_1t_2)}$ for all $t_1, t_2 \\in T$.\n\nLet $X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} = \\{ x_i^{(t)} \\mid i \\in \\mathbb{N}, t \\in T \\}$. Let $F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$ be the free associative algebra on the set of free generators $X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}$. The elements of this algebra are called graded polynomials.\nLet $f=f(x^{(t_1)}_{i_1}, \\dots, x^{(t_s)}_{i_s}) \\in F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$. We say that $f$ is a graded polynomial identity of a $T$-graded algebra $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ and write $f\\equiv 0$ if $f(a^{(t_1)}_{i_1}, \\dots, a^{(t_s)}_{i_s})=0$ for all $a^{(t_j)}_{i_j} \\in A^{(t_j)}$, $1 \\leqslant j \\leqslant s$. The set $Id^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}(A)$ of graded polynomial identities of $A$ is a graded ideal of $F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$.\n\nLet $P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n := \\langle x^{(t_1)}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{(t_2)}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{(t_n)}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid t_i \\in T, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$, $n \\in \\mathbb N$. Then the number $$c^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n(A):=\\dim\\left(\\frac{P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n}{P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n \\cap Id^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}(A)}\\right)$$ is called the $n$th codimension of graded polynomial identities or the $n$th graded codimension of $A$.\n\nLet $H$ be an arbitrary associative algebra with $1$ over a field $F$. Let $F \\langle X \\rangle$ be the free associative algebra without $1$ on the set $X := \\lbrace x_1, x_2, x_3, \\ldots \\rbrace$. Then $F \\langle X \\rangle = \\bigoplus_{n=1}^\\infty F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$ where $F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$ is the linear span of all monomials of total degree $n$. Consider the algebra $$F \\langle X | H\\rangle := \\bigoplus_{n=1}^\\infty H^{\\otimes n} \\otimes F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$$ with the multiplication $(u_1 \\otimes w_1)(u_2 \\otimes w_2):=(u_1 \\otimes u_2) \\otimes w_1w_2$ for all $u_1 \\in H^{\\otimes j}$, $u_2 \\in H^{\\otimes k}$, $w_1 \\in F \\langle X \\rangle^{(j)}$, $w_2 \\in F \\langle X \\rangle^{(k)}$. We use the notation $$x^{h_1}_{i_1} x^{h_2}_{i_2}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{i_n} := (h_1 \\otimes h_2 \\otimes \\ldots \\otimes h_n) \\otimes x_{i_1} x_{i_2}\\ldots x_{i_n}.$$ We refer to the elements of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ as associative $H$-polynomials.\n\nLet $A$ be an associative algebra. Suppose there is a homomorphism $H \\to End_F(A)$, which we denote by $h \\mapsto (a \\mapsto ha)$. Any map $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$ has the unique homomorphic extension $\\bar\\psi \\colon F \\langle X | H \\rangle \\to A$ such that $\\bar\\psi(x_i^h)=h\\psi(x_i)$ for all $i \\in \\mathbb N$ and $h \\in H$. An $H$-polynomial $f \\in F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ is an $H$-identity of $A$ if $\\bar\\psi(f)=0$ for all maps $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$. The set $Id^{H}(A)$ of all $H$-identities of $A$ is an ideal of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$.\n\nWe denote by $P^H_n$ the space of all multilinear $H$-polynomials in $x_1, \\ldots, x_n$, $n\\in\\mathbb N$, i.e. $$P^{H}_n = \\langle x^{h_1}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{h_2}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid h_i \\in H, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X | H \\rangle.$$ Then the number $c^H_n(A):=\\dim\\left(\\frac{P^H_n}{P^H_n \\cap Id^H(A)}\\right)$ is called the $n$th codimension of polynomial $H$-identities or the $n$th $H$-codimension of $A$.\n\nFor an arbitrary semigroup $T$ one can consider the semigroup algebra $FT$ over a field $F$ which is the vector space with the formal basis $(t)_{t\\in T}$ and the multiplication induced by the one in $T$. Consider the vector space $(FT)^*$ dual to $FT$. Then $(FT)^*$ is an algebra with the multiplication defined by $(hw)(t)=h(t)w(t)$ for $h,w \\in (FT)^*$ and $t\\in T$.\n\nLet $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ be a $T$-graded algebra. We have the following natural $(FT)^*$-action on $A$: $h a^{(t)}:=h(t)a^{(t)}$ for all $h \\in (FT)^*$, $a^{(t)}\\in A^{(t)}$ and $t\\in T$. For any $a \\in A$, if we write $a = \\sum_{t \\in T} a^{(t)}$ where $a^{(t)} \\in A^{(t)}$, the action is extended linearly: $ha = \\sum_{t \\in T} h(a^{(t)}) = \\sum_{t \\in T} h(t)a^{(t)}$.\n\nFor every $t\\in T$ define $h_t \\in (FT)^*$ by $h_t(g)=\\left\\lbrace \\begin{array}{ll} 1 & \\text{if } g = t, \\\\ 0 & \\text{if } g\\ne t, \\end{array}\\right.$ for $g\\in T$. If $A$ is finite dimensional, the set $supp \\Gamma := \\lbrace t\\in T \\mid A^{(t)}\\ne 0 \\rbrace$ is finite. For any $a \\in A$, we have $a = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} a^{(t)}$, where $a^{(t)}$ is the projection of $a$ onto $A^{(t)}$. Note that $h_t a = a^{(t)}$.\nFor any $h \\in (FT)^*$ and $a \\in A$, we have $ha = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} h(t) a^{(t)} = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} h(t) h_t a$.\nThis implies that for any $h \\in (FT)^*$, the polynomial $x^h - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) x^{h_t}$ is an $(FT)^*$-identity of $A$. Indeed, for any substitution $x \\to a \\in A$, its evaluation is $ha - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) h_t a = 0$. We denote this as:\n$$\nx^h - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) x^{h_t} \\in Id^{(FT)^*}(A) \\quad (*).\n$$\n## Theorem\nLet $A$ be a finite dimensional algebra over a field $F$ graded by a semigroup $T$. If you are unable to complete the proof, be honest and say so.\n\n## Setup\nLet $F$ be a field. Let $T$ be a semigroup. An associative algebra $A$ is $T$-graded if $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ as a vector space, and $A^{(t_1)}A^{(t_2)} \\subseteq A^{(t_1t_2)}$ for all $t_1, t_2 \\in T$.\n\nLet $X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} = \\{ x_i^{(t)} \\mid i \\in \\mathbb{N}, t \\in T \\}$. Let $F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$ be the free associative algebra on the set of free generators $X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}$. The elements of this algebra are called graded polynomials.\nLet $f=f(x^{(t_1)}_{i_1}, \\dots, x^{(t_s)}_{i_s}) \\in F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$. We say that $f$ is a graded polynomial identity of a $T$-graded algebra $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ and write $f\\equiv 0$ if $f(a^{(t_1)}_{i_1}, \\dots, a^{(t_s)}_{i_s})=0$ for all $a^{(t_j)}_{i_j} \\in A^{(t_j)}$, $1 \\leqslant j \\leqslant s$. The set $Id^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}(A)$ of graded polynomial identities of $A$ is a graded ideal of $F\\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$.\n\nLet $P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n := \\langle x^{(t_1)}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{(t_2)}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{(t_n)}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid t_i \\in T, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}} \\rangle$, $n \\in \\mathbb N$. Then the number $$c^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n(A):=\\dim\\left(\\frac{P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n}{P^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}_n \\cap Id^{T\\text{-}\\mathrm{gr}}(A)}\\right)$$ is called the $n$th codimension of graded polynomial identities or the $n$th graded codimension of $A$.\n\nLet $H$ be an arbitrary associative algebra with $1$ over a field $F$. Let $F \\langle X \\rangle$ be the free associative algebra without $1$ on the set $X := \\lbrace x_1, x_2, x_3, \\ldots \\rbrace$. Then $F \\langle X \\rangle = \\bigoplus_{n=1}^\\infty F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$ where $F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$ is the linear span of all monomials of total degree $n$. Consider the algebra $$F \\langle X | H\\rangle := \\bigoplus_{n=1}^\\infty H^{\\otimes n} \\otimes F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$$ with the multiplication $(u_1 \\otimes w_1)(u_2 \\otimes w_2):=(u_1 \\otimes u_2) \\otimes w_1w_2$ for all $u_1 \\in H^{\\otimes j}$, $u_2 \\in H^{\\otimes k}$, $w_1 \\in F \\langle X \\rangle^{(j)}$, $w_2 \\in F \\langle X \\rangle^{(k)}$. We use the notation $$x^{h_1}_{i_1} x^{h_2}_{i_2}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{i_n} := (h_1 \\otimes h_2 \\otimes \\ldots \\otimes h_n) \\otimes x_{i_1} x_{i_2}\\ldots x_{i_n}.$$ We refer to the elements of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ as associative $H$-polynomials.\n\nLet $A$ be an associative algebra. Suppose there is a homomorphism $H \\to End_F(A)$, which we denote by $h \\mapsto (a \\mapsto ha)$. Any map $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$ has the unique homomorphic extension $\\bar\\psi \\colon F \\langle X | H \\rangle \\to A$ such that $\\bar\\psi(x_i^h)=h\\psi(x_i)$ for all $i \\in \\mathbb N$ and $h \\in H$. An $H$-polynomial $f \\in F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ is an $H$-identity of $A$ if $\\bar\\psi(f)=0$ for all maps $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$. The set $Id^{H}(A)$ of all $H$-identities of $A$ is an ideal of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$.\n\nWe denote by $P^H_n$ the space of all multilinear $H$-polynomials in $x_1, \\ldots, x_n$, $n\\in\\mathbb N$, i.e. $$P^{H}_n = \\langle x^{h_1}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{h_2}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid h_i \\in H, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X | H \\rangle.$$ Then the number $c^H_n(A):=\\dim\\left(\\frac{P^H_n}{P^H_n \\cap Id^H(A)}\\right)$ is called the $n$th codimension of polynomial $H$-identities or the $n$th $H$-codimension of $A$.\n\nFor an arbitrary semigroup $T$ one can consider the semigroup algebra $FT$ over a field $F$ which is the vector space with the formal basis $(t)_{t\\in T}$ and the multiplication induced by the one in $T$. Consider the vector space $(FT)^*$ dual to $FT$. Then $(FT)^*$ is an algebra with the multiplication defined by $(hw)(t)=h(t)w(t)$ for $h,w \\in (FT)^*$ and $t\\in T$.\n\nLet $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ be a $T$-graded algebra. We have the following natural $(FT)^*$-action on $A$: $h a^{(t)}:=h(t)a^{(t)}$ for all $h \\in (FT)^*$, $a^{(t)}\\in A^{(t)}$ and $t\\in T$. For any $a \\in A$, if we write $a = \\sum_{t \\in T} a^{(t)}$ where $a^{(t)} \\in A^{(t)}$, the action is extended linearly: $ha = \\sum_{t \\in T} h(a^{(t)}) = \\sum_{t \\in T} h(t)a^{(t)}$.\n\nFor every $t\\in T$ define $h_t \\in (FT)^*$ by $h_t(g)=\\left\\lbrace \\begin{array}{ll} 1 & \\text{if } g = t, \\\\ 0 & \\text{if } g\\ne t, \\end{array}\\right.$ for $g\\in T$. If $A$ is finite dimensional, the set $supp \\Gamma := \\lbrace t\\in T \\mid A^{(t)}\\ne 0 \\rbrace$ is finite. For any $a \\in A$, we have $a = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} a^{(t)}$, where $a^{(t)}$ is the projection of $a$ onto $A^{(t)}$. Then $F \\langle X \\rangle = \\bigoplus_{n=1}^\\infty F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$ where $F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$ is the linear span of all monomials of total degree $n$. Consider the algebra $$F \\langle X | H\\rangle := \\bigoplus_{n=1}^\\infty H^{\\otimes n} \\otimes F \\langle X \\rangle^{(n)}$$ with the multiplication $(u_1 \\otimes w_1)(u_2 \\otimes w_2):=(u_1 \\otimes u_2) \\otimes w_1w_2$ for all $u_1 \\in H^{\\otimes j}$, $u_2 \\in H^{\\otimes k}$, $w_1 \\in F \\langle X \\rangle^{(j)}$, $w_2 \\in F \\langle X \\rangle^{(k)}$. We use the notation $$x^{h_1}_{i_1} x^{h_2}_{i_2}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{i_n} := (h_1 \\otimes h_2 \\otimes \\ldots \\otimes h_n) \\otimes x_{i_1} x_{i_2}\\ldots x_{i_n}.$$ We refer to the elements of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ as associative $H$-polynomials.\n\nLet $A$ be an associative algebra. Suppose there is a homomorphism $H \\to End_F(A)$, which we denote by $h \\mapsto (a \\mapsto ha)$. Any map $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$ has the unique homomorphic extension $\\bar\\psi \\colon F \\langle X | H \\rangle \\to A$ such that $\\bar\\psi(x_i^h)=h\\psi(x_i)$ for all $i \\in \\mathbb N$ and $h \\in H$. An $H$-polynomial $f \\in F\\langle X | H \\rangle$ is an $H$-identity of $A$ if $\\bar\\psi(f)=0$ for all maps $\\psi \\colon X \\to A$. The set $Id^{H}(A)$ of all $H$-identities of $A$ is an ideal of $F\\langle X | H \\rangle$.\n\nWe denote by $P^H_n$ the space of all multilinear $H$-polynomials in $x_1, \\ldots, x_n$, $n\\in\\mathbb N$, i.e. $$P^{H}_n = \\langle x^{h_1}_{\\sigma(1)} x^{h_2}_{\\sigma(2)}\\ldots x^{h_n}_{\\sigma(n)} \\mid h_i \\in H, \\sigma\\in S_n \\rangle_F \\subset F \\langle X | H \\rangle.$$ Then the number $c^H_n(A):=\\dim\\left(\\frac{P^H_n}{P^H_n \\cap Id^H(A)}\\right)$ is called the $n$th codimension of polynomial $H$-identities or the $n$th $H$-codimension of $A$.\n\nFor an arbitrary semigroup $T$ one can consider the semigroup algebra $FT$ over a field $F$ which is the vector space with the formal basis $(t)_{t\\in T}$ and the multiplication induced by the one in $T$. Consider the vector space $(FT)^*$ dual to $FT$. Then $(FT)^*$ is an algebra with the multiplication defined by $(hw)(t)=h(t)w(t)$ for $h,w \\in (FT)^*$ and $t\\in T$.\n\nLet $A=\\bigoplus_{t\\in T} A^{(t)}$ be a $T$-graded algebra. We have the following natural $(FT)^*$-action on $A$: $h a^{(t)}:=h(t)a^{(t)}$ for all $h \\in (FT)^*$, $a^{(t)}\\in A^{(t)}$ and $t\\in T$. For any $a \\in A$, if we write $a = \\sum_{t \\in T} a^{(t)}$ where $a^{(t)} \\in A^{(t)}$, the action is extended linearly: $ha = \\sum_{t \\in T} h(a^{(t)}) = \\sum_{t \\in T} h(t)a^{(t)}$.\n\nFor every $t\\in T$ define $h_t \\in (FT)^*$ by $h_t(g)=\\left\\lbrace \\begin{array}{ll} 1 & \\text{if } g = t, \\\\ 0 & \\text{if } g\\ne t, \\end{array}\\right.$ for $g\\in T$. If $A$ is finite dimensional, the set $supp \\Gamma := \\lbrace t\\in T \\mid A^{(t)}\\ne 0 \\rbrace$ is finite. For any $a \\in A$, we have $a = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} a^{(t)}$, where $a^{(t)}$ is the projection of $a$ onto $A^{(t)}$. Note that $h_t a = a^{(t)}$.\nFor any $h \\in (FT)^*$ and $a \\in A$, we have $ha = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} h(t) a^{(t)} = \\sum_{t \\in supp \\Gamma} h(t) h_t a$.\nThis implies that for any $h \\in (FT)^*$, the polynomial $x^h - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) x^{h_t}$ is an $(FT)^*$-identity of $A$. Indeed, for any substitution $x \\to a \\in A$, its evaluation is $ha - \\sum_{t\\in supp \\Gamma}h(t) h_t a = 0$. Your job is to provide a complete and correct proof. Think carefully, trying multiple approaches if you are stuck, and finally give a cleaned up proof under the heading ## Proof. If you are unable to complete the proof, be honest and say so.\n\n## Setup\nLet $G$ be a graph and $\\mathcal{H}$ be a hypergraph both on the same vertex set. We say that the hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ is a **Berge-$G$** if there is a bijection\n$f : E(G) \\rightarrow E(\\mathcal{H})$ such that for $e \\in E(G)$ we have $e \\subset f(e)$.\n\nWe say that a hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ contains the graph $G$ if $\\mathcal{H}$ has a Berge-$G$ as a subhypergraph. If $\\mathcal{H}$ contains no $G$, then we say that $\\mathcal{H}$ is **$G$-free**.\n\nWhen considering non-uniform hypergraphs $\\mathcal{H}$ we are interested in the value of $\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})} d(v) = \\sum_{h \\in \\mathcal{H}} |h|$ instead of $|\\mathcal{H}|$. Note that when a hypergraph is uniform than the two parameters can easily be computed from each other.\n\n**Theorem 4 (Kővári-Sós-Turán theorem)**\nIf $G$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free graph on $n$ vertices and $s \\leq t$, then there is a constant $C$ depending on $s$ and $t$ such that\n$$|E(G)| \\leq C n^{2-1/s}.$$\n\n**Lemma 1**\nLet $F$ be a graph and suppose $\\mathcal{H}$ is an $F$-free hypergraph. If every edge of $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $|V(F)|$,\nthen $|\\mathcal{H}| \\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n**Proof of Lemma 1** Let us construct a graph $H$ on the ground set of $\\mathcal{H}$ by embedding a (unique) edge into each hyperedge of $\\mathcal{H}$. By definition if $\\mathcal{H}$ is $F$-free, then $H$ must be $F$-free and thus\n$|\\mathcal{H}|=|H|\\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n\nWe can construct $H$ greedily. Take the hyperedges of $\\mathcal{H}$ in arbitrary order and for each hyperedge embed an edge that has not already been used in $H$.\nIf at some step we cannot find such an edge, then $H$ contains a complete graph of size equal to the size of the hyperedge. Obviously, such a complete graph contains a copy of $F$; a contradiction.\n\n**Lemma 2**\nA properly edge-colored $K_{s,s(s-1)(t-1)+t}$ contains a rainbow $K_{s,t}$.\n**Proof of Lemma 2** Suppose $K_{s,s(s-1)(t-1)+t}$ is properly edge-colored and let $S$ be the class of size $s$ and $T$ be the other class.\nConsider the largest rainbow $K_{s,t'}$ that uses all $s$ vertices of $S$. That is, there are $t'$ vertices in $T$ such that all edges between them and $S$ are of distinct colors. It is enough to show that $t' \\geq t$.\n\nObviously $t' \\ge 1$ as the edge-coloring is proper. For every vertex $x$ not in $K_{s,t'}$ there is a neighbor of $x$ in $S$ such that the color of the edge between them is one of the colors used in $K_{s,t'}$ (otherwise we can add $x$ to $K_{s,t'}$).\nEach vertex $y \\in S$ is adjacent to $t'$ vertices in $K_{s,t'}$, so there are $st'-t' = (s-1)t'$ colors\nthat appear in $K_{s,t'}$ not on edges incident to $y$. So at most $y$ can force $(s-1)t'$ many vertices in $T$ to not be in $K_{s,t'}$.\nTherefore, in total, there are at most $s(s-1)t'$ vertices in $T$ that are not in $K_{s,t'}$.\nSo $T$ has at most $s(s-1)t' + t'$ vertices, hence $t'\\ge t$.\n## Theorem\n**Theorem 5**\nIf $\\mathcal{H}$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph, $s \\le t$ and every hyperedge in $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $s+t$, then\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = O(n^{2-1/s}).$$\nFurthermore, there exists a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ such that each edge has size $s+t$ and\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = \\Omega( \\ex(n,K_{s,t})).$$", 'evaluation_result': {'response_a': {'instruction_following': {'score': 5, 'reason': "The response follows the core instructions: provides a complete proof under '## Proof' heading, attempts both upper and lower bounds as required by Theorem 5. By definition if $\\mathcal{H}$ is $F$-free, then $H$ must be $F$-free and thus\n$|\\mathcal{H}|=|H|\\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n\nWe can construct $H$ greedily. So at most $y$ can force $(s-1)t'$ many vertices in $T$ to not be in $K_{s,t'}$.\nTherefore, in total, there are at most $s(s-1)t'$ vertices in $T$ that are not in $K_{s,t'}$.\nSo $T$ has at most $s(s-1)t' + t'$ vertices, hence $t'\\ge t$.\n## Theorem\n**Theorem 5**\nIf $\\mathcal{H}$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph, $s \\le t$ and every hyperedge in $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $s+t$, then\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = O(n^{2-1/s}).$$\nFurthermore, there exists a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ such that each edge has size $s+t$ and\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = \\Omega( \\ex(n,K_{s,t})).$$", 'evaluation_result': {'response_a': {'instruction_following': {'score': 2, 'reason': "The response fails to complete the core task. 2026-03-09 09:28:17,043 2436895 WARNING grtlabs_db odoo.http: No CSRF validation token provided for path '/'

Odoo URLs are CSRF-protected by default (when accessed with unsafe
HTTP methods). See
https://www.odoo.com/documentation/master/developer/reference/addons/http.html#csrf
for more details.

* if this endpoint is accessed through Odoo via py-QWeb form, embed a CSRF
  token in the form, Tokens are available via `request.csrf_token()`
  can be provided through a hidden input and must be POST-ed named
  `csrf_token` e.g. in your form add:
      <input type="hidden" name="csrf_token" t-att-value="request.csrf_token()"/>

* if the form is generated or posted in javascript, the token value is
  available as `csrf_token` on `web.core` and as the `csrf_token`
  value in the default js-qweb execution context

* if the form is accessed by an external third party (e.g. REST API
  endpoint, payment gateway callback) you will need to disable CSRF
  protection (and implement your own protection if necessary) by
  passing the `csrf=False` parameter to the `route` decorator.

So at most $y$ can force $(s-1)t'$ many vertices in $T$ to not be in $K_{s,t'}$.\nTherefore, in total, there are at most $s(s-1)t'$ vertices in $T$ that are not in $K_{s,t'}$.\nSo $T$ has at most $s(s-1)t' + t'$ vertices, hence $t'\\ge t$.\n## Theorem\n**Theorem 5**\nIf $\\mathcal{H}$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph, $s \\le t$ and every hyperedge in $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $s+t$, then\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = O(n^{2-1/s}).$$\nFurthermore, there exists a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ such that each edge has size $s+t$ and\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = \\Omega( \\ex(n,K_{s,t})).$$", 'evaluation_result': {'response_a': {'instruction_following': {'score': 6, 'reason': 'Complete checklist verification: [X] Provide complete and correct proof - done with full rigor; [X] Think carefully, try multiple approaches - the proof uses sophisticated counting and probabilistic methods; [X] Give cleaned up proof under ## Proof heading - present; [X] Be honest if unable to complete - not needed as proof is complete. Your job is to provide a complete and correct proof. Think carefully, trying multiple approaches if you are stuck, and finally give a cleaned up proof under the heading ## Proof. If you are unable to complete the proof, be honest and say so.\n\n## Setup\nLet $G$ be a graph and $\\mathcal{H}$ be a hypergraph both on the same vertex set. We say that the hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ is a **Berge-$G$** if there is a bijection\n$f : E(G) \\rightarrow E(\\mathcal{H})$ such that for $e \\in E(G)$ we have $e \\subset f(e)$.\n\nWe say that a hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ contains the graph $G$ if $\\mathcal{H}$ has a Berge-$G$ as a subhypergraph. If $\\mathcal{H}$ contains no $G$, then we say that $\\mathcal{H}$ is **$G$-free**.\n\nWhen considering non-uniform hypergraphs $\\mathcal{H}$ we are interested in the value of $\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})} d(v) = \\sum_{h \\in \\mathcal{H}} |h|$ instead of $|\\mathcal{H}|$. Note that when a hypergraph is uniform than the two parameters can easily be computed from each other.\n\n**Theorem 4 (Kővári-Sós-Turán theorem)**\nIf $G$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free graph on $n$ vertices and $s \\leq t$, then there is a constant $C$ depending on $s$ and $t$ such that\n$$|E(G)| \\leq C n^{2-1/s}.$$\n\n**Lemma 1**\nLet $F$ be a graph and suppose $\\mathcal{H}$ is an $F$-free hypergraph. If every edge of $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $|V(F)|$,\nthen $|\\mathcal{H}| \\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n**Proof of Lemma 1** Let us construct a graph $H$ on the ground set of $\\mathcal{H}$ by embedding a (unique) edge into each hyperedge of $\\mathcal{H}$. By definition if $\\mathcal{H}$ is $F$-free, then $H$ must be $F$-free and thus\n$|\\mathcal{H}|=|H|\\le \\ex(n,F)$.\n\nWe can construct $H$ greedily. Take the hyperedges of $\\mathcal{H}$ in arbitrary order and for each hyperedge embed an edge that has not already been used in $H$.\nIf at some step we cannot find such an edge, then $H$ contains a complete graph of size equal to the size of the hyperedge. Obviously, such a complete graph contains a copy of $F$; a contradiction.\n\n**Lemma 2**\nA properly edge-colored $K_{s,s(s-1)(t-1)+t}$ contains a rainbow $K_{s,t}$.\n**Proof of Lemma 2** Suppose $K_{s,s(s-1)(t-1)+t}$ is properly edge-colored and let $S$ be the class of size $s$ and $T$ be the other class.\nConsider the largest rainbow $K_{s,t'}$ that uses all $s$ vertices of $S$. That is, there are $t'$ vertices in $T$ such that all edges between them and $S$ are of distinct colors. It is enough to show that $t' \\geq t$.\n\nObviously $t' \\ge 1$ as the edge-coloring is proper. For every vertex $x$ not in $K_{s,t'}$ there is a neighbor of $x$ in $S$ such that the color of the edge between them is one of the colors used in $K_{s,t'}$ (otherwise we can add $x$ to $K_{s,t'}$).\nEach vertex $y \\in S$ is adjacent to $t'$ vertices in $K_{s,t'}$, so there are $st'-t' = (s-1)t'$ colors\nthat appear in $K_{s,t'}$ not on edges incident to $y$. So at most $y$ can force $(s-1)t'$ many vertices in $T$ to not be in $K_{s,t'}$.\nTherefore, in total, there are at most $s(s-1)t'$ vertices in $T$ that are not in $K_{s,t'}$.\nSo $T$ has at most $s(s-1)t' + t'$ vertices, hence $t'\\ge t$.\n## Theorem\n**Theorem 5**\nIf $\\mathcal{H}$ is a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph, $s \\le t$ and every hyperedge in $\\mathcal{H}$ has size at least $s+t$, then\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = O(n^{2-1/s}).$$\nFurthermore, there exists a $K_{s,t}$-free hypergraph $\\mathcal{H}$ such that each edge has size $s+t$ and\n$$\\sum_{v \\in V(\\mathcal{H})}d(v) = \\Omega( \\ex(n,K_{s,t})).$$", 'evaluation_result': {'response_a': {'instruction_following': {'score': 6, 'reason': 'Complete checklist verification: [X] Provides complete proof under ## Proof heading; [X] Proves upper bound O(n^{2-1/s}); [X] Proves lower bound construction with Ω(ex(n,K_{s,t})); [X] Uses given lemmas (Lemma 1, Lemma 2); [X] Handles condition that every hyperedge has size at least s+t; [X] No unrequested content added.
